That number is a **generation limit, not an evaluation protocol**: it is how\n    23\tmany worlds the environment can build, not the set anybody scores on.\n    24\t\n    25\tThe evaluation protocol is in the repository's own `goldpaths/goldpaths-all.zip`, which\n    26\tmarks every reference solution with a `train` / `dev` / `test` fold:\n    27\t\n    28\t    measured over the archive: 6,907 paths\n    29\t      train 3,442   dev 1,721   test 1,744\n    30\t      test paths span all 29 task types that have gold paths\n    31\t\n    32\tThis adapter returns **the `test` fold**, one task per variation. The alternative -- every variation the\n    35\tenvironment can generate -- mixes the folds the benchmark separated and inflates the task\n    36\tcount with worlds the evaluation never uses.\n    37\t\n    38\t**The fold's identity is transcribed into git, not fetched.** `raw.githubusercontent.com` is\n    39\tblocked on this host and a branch URL is not a pin, so the archive itself cannot be\n    40\tprovisioned reproducibly; `data/datasets/scienceworld/test_fold.json` carries the 1,744\n    41\t`(task_name, variation)` pairs and each path's length, with the archive's sha256 recorded\n    42\tbeside them, and a test rebuilds the file from the archive and fails if the two disagree.\n    43\tOnly the *identity* of the tasks is needed at run time: the gold action sequences are\n    44\tregenerated from the environment's own API, which is what makes the oracle control test the\n    45\t**environment** rather than a stored answer.\n    46\t\n    47\tThe adapter keeps the complete `test` fold at the paper's 100-step budget (\"we used\n    48\t100 in all experiments\"). **Limit: long oracle paths truncate at 100**, including\n    49\t299 archived test paths longer than 100 moves; the longest is 796. Reference length\n    50\tdoes not filter the evaluation pool. `fold_over_budget()` reports these paths.\n    51\t\n    52\tOne discrepancy remains visible in the numbers: the package enumerates **30** task names\n    53\tand the archive has gold paths for **29**. The missing type is\n    54\t`measure-melting-point-unknown-substance` (300 variations, the 7,207 - 6,907 gap).\n    55\t`identify-life-stages-2` is present as `task-7-identify-life-stages-2/6` through `/9`.\n    56\tThe released-fold macro therefore covers **29/30 task types** at most.\n    57\t\n    58\tThe variation indices in the archive are the package's own (verified: the API's\n    59\t`load(task, variationIdx, ...)` on a test-fold index reproduces that fold's path\n    60\tcharacter for character -- `tests/quality/test_scienceworld_dataset.py` pins the comparison\n    61\ton real entries), so the fold and the environment agree about what a task *is*.\n    62\t\n    63\tThe metric, and what is deliberately not added\n    64\t----------------------------------------------\n    65\t`reward = max(0, score) / 100.0`, where `score` is the **last** `info['score']` the\n    66\tenvironment reported, an integer 0-100. That is what the environment's own evaluation loop\n    67\trecords (`examples/random_agent.py`: `score = info['score']`, appended per episode; its\n    68\tsummary clips negatives before averaging, which is where `max(0, ...)` comes from). The\n    69\tinteger score is carried in the evidence beside the fraction, and the goal-met predicate is\n    70\t`score == 100`.\n    71\t\n    72\tThe platform's `score` is the micro mean over measured variations. The paper's Table 2\n    73\t\"Average\" is an unweighted macro over 30 task types. `aggregate(point[\"per_task\"])`\n    74\treports both micro and macro over observed task types, per-type means and coverage.\n    75\tIts `corpus_metrics` policy is declared in `data/aggregation_policies.json`, following\n    76\tthe existing MuSiQue/2Wiki corpus-metric route. A partial result uses only observed types;\n    77\tunmeasured tasks and types are not zeroes. Even a complete released-fold result has\n    78\t29/30 types and must not be called the paper's full 30-type Average. The report includes\n    79\tthe 100-step truncation limit beside both numbers. To export a curve point from a\n    80\trun known to have used 100 steps (omit `--step-budget` when its budget is unknown):\n    81\t\n    82\t    python3 -m data.datasets.scienceworld --aggregate point.json --step-budget 100\n    83\t\n    84\tChanging the default does not change the budget of retained historical scores.\n    85\t\n    86\t`scw done` exists because a harness needs a way to stop; it is recorded and it is **not** a\n    87\tgate, because ScienceWorld has no such requirement (`docs/dataset-onboarding-skill.md` §2).\n    88\t\n    89\tTwo decisions a reader should know about\n    90\t----------------------------------------\n    91\t* **No simplifications.** `simplificationStr` is empty, so the worlds are the full ones.\n    92\t  The paper also reports an \"easy\" preset (`teleportAction`, `openDoors`,\n    93\t  `selfWateringFlowerPots`, `noElectricalAction`), which is a *different* task set with a\n    94\t  different score; mixing the two in one curve is the error this note exists to prevent.\n    95\t* **A 100-move budget.** This follows the paper's \"we used 100 in all experiments\".\n    96\t  Long oracle paths truncate at 100; their tasks remain in the evaluation pool.\n    97\t  The episode stops when the goal is met or the budget is spent. `STEP_BUDGET` is declared\n    98\t  once in `lib/scw_env.py` and imported by the CLI, check and control, so the three cannot\n    99\t  drift apart.\n   100\t\n   101\tThe leak boundary\n   102\t-----------------\n   103\t`load(..., generateGoldPath=True)` and `get_gold_action_sequence()` make the reference\n"
